- c. Ongoing monitoring commensurate with risk
- d. Identical due diligence for every customer regardless of risk

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** A risk-based approach varies the intensity of controls with assessed risk. Uniform treatment regardless of risk is the opposite of a risk-based approach.

</details>

### 393. **[Single choice]** [RES 1B §7.9] A customer repeatedly structures deposits just below the reporting threshold. This is best described as:

- a. Structuring, a recognised suspicious transaction indicator
- b. A legitimate cash management technique
- c. Tax avoidance
- d. A breach of the Securities and Futures Act

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Structuring, a recognised suspicious transaction indicator

**Rationale:** Structuring seeks to avoid thresholds and is a classic placement indicator. It is a money laundering concern rather than a legitimate technique.

</details>

### 394. **[Scenario]** [RES 1B §3.20] A representative places matched buy and sell orders in the same contract month for accounts he controls, at the same price, to create volume. Which offence best describes this?

- a. False trading by means of a wash trade
- b. Bucketing
- c. Churning
- d. Front running

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. False trading by means of a wash trade

**Rationale:** Matched orders with no genuine change in beneficial ownership and no economic purpose are a wash trade, a form of false trading. Churning concerns commission generation; front running concerns trading ahead of a client.

</details>

### 395. **[Scenario]** [RES 1B §3.20] A representative, learning that a client is about to place a large buy order, purchases for a personal account first. Which offence best describes this?

- a. Front running, breaching the priority of customer orders
- b. False trading
- c. Cornering
- d. Dissemination of information about illegal transactions

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Front running, breaching the priority of customer orders

**Rationale:** Trading ahead of a known client order misuses non-public order information and breaches the obligation to give customer orders priority.

</details>

### 396. **[Scenario]** [RES 1B §7.9] A client asks a representative why a large transfer to an unrelated overseas party was queried by the firm. The representative should:

- a. Give a neutral explanation that queries are routine, without revealing that a suspicious transaction report may have been filed
- b. Explain that a suspicious transaction report has been filed
- c. Refuse to speak to the client at all
- d. Promise that no report will be made

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Give a neutral explanation that queries are routine, without revealing that a suspicious transaction report may have been filed

**Rationale:** Disclosing that a report has been made would be tipping off. A neutral response protects both the client relationship and the integrity of the reporting regime.

</details>

### 397. **[Single choice]** [RES 1B §6.5] A member applying for a CPFIS Investment Account is asked which banks will accept the application and how many accounts may be held. The correct answer is:

- a. DBS, OCBC or UOB, and only one account at a time
- b. DBS, OCBC or UOB, and one account with each bank
- c. Any MAS-licensed bank, and only one account at a time
- d. Any CPFIS-included bank, and up to two accounts

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. DBS, OCBC or UOB, and only one account at a time

**Rationale:** There are three agent banks — DBS, OCBC and UOB — and a member may hold only one CPF Investment Account at any time, regardless of how many banks offer the service.

</details>

### 398. **[Multiple response]** [RES 1B §6.5] A member attends a branch to open a CPFIS Investment Account. Which items does the guide describe the member as bringing for verification?

- a. An identity card
- b. The SAQ status document
- c. A CPF statement so the bank can verify the CPF account number
- d. A statutory declaration of all existing investments

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** Identity, SAQ status and a CPF statement are the three items named. No statutory declaration of existing investments is required.

</details>

### 399. **[Single choice]** [RES 1B §6.8] Which CPFIS investments require no application by the product provider and no review by the CPF Board?

- a. Singapore Government Bonds, Treasury Bills and gold products offered by CPF agent banks
- b. Unit trusts and exchange-traded funds
- c. Fixed deposits and statutory board bonds
- d. Annuities and investment-linked policies

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Singapore Government Bonds, Treasury Bills and gold products offered by CPF agent banks

**Rationale:** Government bonds, T-bills and agent-bank gold products are the stated exceptions. Everything else is included only on the provider’s application and after CPF Board review, with unit trusts, ILPs and ETFs additionally evaluated by the Board’s appointed investment consultant.

</details>

### 400. **[Single choice]** [RES 1B §6.8] An investor proposes to pledge CPFIS-included shares as security for a bank loan. Is that permitted?

- a. No, because CPFIS investments cannot be assigned, pledged or used as collateral
- b. Yes, provided the loan is taken from a CPFIS-included bank
- c. Yes, provided the member has set aside the Full Retirement Sum
- d. Yes, but only after the investment account has been open for one year

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. No, because CPFIS investments cannot be assigned, pledged or used as collateral

**Rationale:** CPFIS investments cannot be assigned, pledged or used as collateral. The restriction is absolute and is not cured by the identity of the lender or by the member’s retirement sum.

</details>

### 401. **[Single choice]** [RES 1B §6.10] A member receives dividends on CPFIS-OA shares. What may the member do with that money?

- a. It cannot be withdrawn as cash, but may be used for other CPF schemes subject to their terms
- b. It may be withdrawn as cash from the agent bank at any time
- c. It must be reinvested in the same counter within 30 days
- d. It is automatically transferred to the Special Account

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. It cannot be withdrawn as cash, but may be used for other CPF schemes subject to their terms

**Rationale:** Dividends and profits cannot be withdrawn as cash because the scheme exists to grow retirement savings. They may be applied to other CPF schemes on those schemes’ terms.

</details>

### 402. **[Single choice]** [RES 1B §6.10] A CPFIS investment loses 30% of its value. What does the member owe to the CPF account?

- a. Nothing, because losses do not need to be made good
- b. The full loss, restored in cash on realisation
- c. The loss, but only up to the amount of dividends received
- d. The loss, deducted from the Retirement Account

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Nothing, because losses do not need to be made good

**Rationale:** Losses do not have to be made good. The member bears the investment risk; there is no restitution obligation back to the CPF account.

</details>

### 403. **[Single choice]** [RES 1B §6.11] What is the current cap on a wrap fee charged through CPFIS, following the October 2020 changes?

- a. 0.4%
- b. 0.7%
- c. 1.0%
- d. There is no cap

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. 0.4%

**Rationale:** October 2020 reduced the wrap fee cap to 0.4% and removed the sales charge cap altogether. The earlier figures were 1.0% and 1.5% (from October 2018) and 1% and 3% before that.

</details>

### 404. **[Multiple response]** [RES 1B §6.11] Which statements about CPFIS charges are correct?

- a. Charges may be paid from CPF savings
- b. The agent bank may levy charges for the account and for settlement and accounting
- c. The sales charge cap had been removed by October 2020
- d. The wrap fee cap was removed in October 2020

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** The sales charge cap was removed and the wrap fee cap was reduced to 0.4% — it was not removed. Charges may be paid from CPF savings and may be levied by product and service providers as well as by the agent bank.

</details>

### 405. **[Single choice]** [RES 1B §6.13] An undischarged bankrupt holds existing CPFIS investments. Which statement is correct?

- a. The member may hold or liquidate existing investments but may not make new investments with CPF money
- b. The member may neither hold nor liquidate the investments while undischarged
- c. The member may make new investments provided the Official Assignee consents
- d. The investments vest in the Official Assignee on the making of the bankruptcy order

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. The member may hold or liquidate existing investments but may not make new investments with CPF money

**Rationale:** Existing holdings may be held or liquidated, with proceeds credited back to the CPF Investment Account or Special Account. New investments using CPF money are prohibited.

</details>

### 406. **[Single choice]** [RES 1B §6.13] What protection do CPFIS investments and investment-account cash have while they remain within the scheme?

- a. They are protected from creditors and the Official Assignee
- b. They are protected only after the member reaches 55
- c. They are protected only if the member has set aside the Full Retirement Sum
- d. They are unprotected and form part of the bankrupt estate

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. They are protected from creditors and the Official Assignee

**Rationale:** CPFIS investments and investment-account cash are protected from creditors and the Official Assignee while within the scheme. At 55 the FRS or BRS requirement still governs withdrawal, which is a separate question from creditor protection.

</details>

### 407. **[Single choice]** [RES 1B §6.14] A member dies holding CPFIS investments. What is the effect of a CPF nomination made under the CPF Act?

- a. It does not cover CPFIS investments, which form part of the estate
- b. It transfers the CPFIS investments to the nominated beneficiaries directly
- c. It transfers the investment-account cash only, and not the investments
- d. It has effect only if the nomination is irrevocable

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. It does not cover CPFIS investments, which form part of the estate

**Rationale:** CPFIS investments and investment-account cash are not covered by CPF nomination and form part of the estate. The administrator or executor must produce Letters of Administration or a Grant of Probate to claim them.

</details>

### 408. **[Multiple response]** [RES 1B §6.14] Which statements about CPFIS insurance policies on the member’s death are correct?

- a. A revocable nomination made with the insurer pays the nominated beneficiaries directly
- b. Without a nomination, the proceeds are distributed with the rest of the estate
- c. Irrevocable nomination is not allowed for CPFIS insurance policies
- d. On death the CPFIS assets remain protected from the deceased’s creditors

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** Revocable nomination bypasses the estate, and without one the proceeds fall into the estate. Irrevocable nomination is not permitted. The protection ends on death, so the assets may satisfy the deceased’s creditors under the Probate and Administration Act.

</details>

## RES 2B — Extension

### 409. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §2.2] A corporation dealing in exchange-traded derivatives as a clearing member must maintain base capital of at least:

- a. S$5 million
- b. S$1 million
- c. S$500,000
- d. S$50,000

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. S$5 million

**Rationale:** A clearing member for exchange-traded derivatives requires S$5 million, the highest tier in that table.

</details>

### 410. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §2.2] A non-clearing member dealing in OTC derivatives with retail clients requires base capital of:

- a. S$5 million
- b. S$1 million
- c. S$500,000
- d. S$250,000

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. S$5 million

**Rationale:** The retail distinction doubles the requirement: S$5 million with retail clients against S$1 million for non-retail only.

</details>

### 411. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §2.2] A restricted broker dealing only in specified commodity futures contracts requires base capital of:

- a. S$250,000
- b. S$500,000
- c. S$1 million
- d. S$5 million

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. S$250,000

**Rationale:** The restricted broker and the introducing broker both sit at S$250,000 in the commodity futures table.

</details>

### 412. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §2.2] Which statements about the board and senior management of a CMS licence applicant are correct?

- a. The board must have at least two members
- b. At least one board member must be resident in Singapore
- c. The chief executive officer must be resident in Singapore
- d. MAS approval is required to change a director’s appointment from non-executive to executive

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C, D

**Rationale:** All four conditions apply, including the requirement for MAS approval when a non-executive directorship becomes executive.

</details>

### 413. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §2.2] A CMS licence holder must inform MAS of a change in the address of its principal place of business within:

- a. 14 days
- b. 7 days
- c. 30 days
- d. 3 months

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. 14 days

**Rationale:** The notification period for changes in the holder’s particulars is 14 days, because the information is published to the investing community.

</details>

### 414. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §2.2] Which are duties of a CMS licence holder under the internal control requirements?

- a. Implementing effective written policies across all operational areas
- b. Setting out in writing the limits of discretionary powers of officers and committees
- c. Ensuring effective control and segregation of duties
- d. Delegating all compliance responsibility to the front office

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** Written policies, documented discretionary limits and segregation of duties are prescribed. Delegating compliance to the front office defeats the segregation the rules require.

</details>

### 415. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §2.6] Which statements about the monthly statement for a derivatives account are correct?

- a. It must show outstanding positions, the prices at which they were established and net unrealised profit or loss marked to market
- b. It must show the status of every asset held in custody
- c. It must show the movement and balance of money in the account
- d. It must disclose the counterparties to each OTC trade

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** Positions, custody status, movements and charges are all required. Naming OTC counterparties is not a statement requirement and would raise confidentiality issues.

</details>

### 416. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §2.6] For an OTC derivatives transaction, the CMS licence holder may avoid issuing a contract note if it provides a confirmation stating:

- a. The name of the holder, the name of the recipient, principal capacity where applicable, the trade date and the type and notional value
- b. Only the trade date
- c. Only the counterparty name and the commission
- d. The full terms of the ISDA Master Agreement

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. The name of the holder, the name of the recipient, principal capacity where applicable, the trade date and the type and notional value

**Rationale:** Five elements make up the permitted confirmation: holder, recipient, principal capacity, date, and type and notional value.

</details>

### 417. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §2.8] Which are permissible depositories for a trust or custody account?

- a. A bank or merchant bank licensed under the Banking Act
- b. A finance company licensed under the Finance Companies Act
- c. An approved trustee for a collective investment scheme
- d. Any unregulated overseas entity nominated by the customer

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** The permitted institutions are licensed banks, merchant banks, finance companies, depository agents and approved trustees, plus persons licensed to provide custodial services. An unregulated entity is not permitted.

</details>

### 418. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §2.8] Where a CMS licence holder opens a trust account with a custodian outside Singapore, it must:

- a. Give written notice to the custodian and obtain a similar acknowledgement that the assets are held on trust and cannot be used to offset the holder’s debts
- b. Rely on the overseas custodian’s standard terms
- c. Obtain MAS approval for each account
- d. Notify the customer only at year end

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Give written notice to the custodian and obtain a similar acknowledgement that the assets are held on trust and cannot be used to offset the holder’s debts

**Rationale:** The acknowledgement requirement applies equally to an overseas custodian: the assets must be ring-fenced from the holder’s own debts.

</details>

### 419. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §2.8] For moneys held in a customer trust account, interest earned and returns from permitted investments:

- a. Accrue to the customer
- b. Belong to the CMS licence holder
- c. Are shared equally
- d. Are paid to MAS

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Accrue to the customer

**Rationale:** Interest and investment returns on customer money accrue to the customer; the holder may retain only the proportionate return on its own money advanced to the account.

</details>

### 420. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §3.2] A person in Tokyo manipulates the price of a futures contract listed on a Singapore exchange. For the purposes of the market conduct provisions, the act is:

- a. Regarded as having taken place in Singapore
- b. Outside the reach of the SFA because it occurred abroad
- c. Governed only by Japanese law
- d. Not actionable unless the person is resident in Singapore

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Regarded as having taken place in Singapore

**Rationale:** The provisions have extraterritorial reach: acts outside Singapore affecting contracts listed or quoted on an organised market in Singapore are caught.

</details>

### 421. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §3.2] Which of the following are securities-based derivatives contracts?

- a. Futures on an equity index
- b. Options on an equity index
- c. A commodity swap
- d. Structured warrants on a listed share

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, D

**Rationale:** A securities-based derivative has a security or securities index as its underlying. A commodity swap has a commodity underlying.

</details>

### 422. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §3.3] Which element distinguishes false trading from a legitimate trade?

- a. The absence of a genuine investment objective and the intention to create a false impression of activity or price
- b. The size of the trade
- c. Whether the trade is profitable
- d. Whether the trade is cleared

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. The absence of a genuine investment objective and the intention to create a false impression of activity or price

**Rationale:** False trading is defined by purpose and effect: no genuine investment objective, and an intention to create an appearance of activity or to move the price away from its natural level.

</details>

### 423. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §3.10] Which are effective defences to a charge of insider trading?

- a. The information had been made known in a manner likely to bring it to the attention of persons who commonly invest
- b. The counterparty knew or ought reasonably to have known of the information before the transaction
- c. The trade was small relative to the person’s portfolio
- d. The person did not intend to make a profit

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B

**Rationale:** The two statutory defences turn on prior dissemination and on the counterparty’s own knowledge. Size and absence of intent are not defences, and intention need not be proved.

</details>

### 424. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §3.14] A CMS licence holder wishes to take the opposite side of a customer’s futures order. It may do so only:

- a. With the customer’s prior consent and in accordance with the business rules of the exchange
- b. If the trade is profitable for the customer
- c. If the amount is below a threshold
- d. Without any condition, because it is a principal

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. With the customer’s prior consent and in accordance with the business rules of the exchange

**Rationale:** Trading against a customer requires prior consent plus compliance with exchange business rules; the capacity of principal does not by itself authorise it.

</details>

### 425. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §3.17] Which statements about churning and overtrading are correct?

- a. Churning is executing trades to generate commission without sound market opportunities
- b. Overtrading includes maintaining positions that breach limits imposed by an exchange or clearing house
- c. A holder is responsible for keeping customer positions within prescribed limits
- d. Churning is permitted if the customer signs a general risk disclosure

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** Churning is defined by the purpose of generating commission, and position limits bind both proprietary and customer positions. A risk disclosure does not authorise churning.

</details>

### 426. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §3.19] Pre-arranged trading at an agreed price between dealers is prohibited because it:

- a. Violates the principle of fair and transparent markets by letting selected participants benefit from premeditated prices
- b. Is always unprofitable
- c. Requires MAS approval that is never granted
- d. Breaches clearing-house margin rules

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Violates the principle of fair and transparent markets by letting selected participants benefit from premeditated prices

**Rationale:** The objection is to the unfair advantage obtained over other participants, not to profitability, approval or margin.

</details>

### 427. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §4.2] Which of the following are "specified persons" for the OTC derivatives reporting regime?

- a. Banks and merchant banks
- b. Finance companies and insurers
- c. Holders of a capital markets services licence
- d. Retail investors who are accredited investors

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** Specified persons are the regulated institutions and CMS licence holders. Retail and accredited investors are not specified persons.

</details>

### 428. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §4.2] Under section 129C of the SFA, a specified person who is party to a specified derivatives contract must:

- a. Clear the contract with an approved or recognised clearing house
- b. Report the contract to MAS
- c. Obtain MAS approval before trading
- d. Post collateral with a licensed bank

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Clear the contract with an approved or recognised clearing house

**Rationale:** Section 129C imposes the clearing obligation. Reporting is a separate obligation under Part 6A and goes to a trade repository.

</details>

### 429. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §4.2] Which asset classes of derivatives are prescribed for the reporting regime when traded or booked in Singapore?

- a. Interest rate derivatives
- b. Credit derivatives
- c. Foreign exchange derivatives
- d. Equity and commodity derivatives

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C, D

**Rationale:** All five prescribed classes report: interest rate, credit, foreign exchange, commodity and equity derivatives.

</details>

### 430. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §4.3] OTC commodity derivatives are registered for clearing through:

- a. The Titan OTC system
- b. CDP
- c. The SGX-ST trading engine
- d. MEPS+

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. The Titan OTC system

**Rationale:** SGX-DC clears OTC commodity derivatives as non-relevant market contracts, registered through Titan OTC.

</details>

### 431. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §4.4] Which form is the prescribed derivatives risk disclosure statement?

- a. Form 13
- b. Form 14
- c. Form 1
- d. Form 8

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Form 13

**Rationale:** Form 13 is the derivatives risk disclosure; Form 14 is the risk warning for overseas-listed investment products.

</details>

### 432. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §4.5] Which terms are typically addressed in an ISDA Master Agreement?

- a. Governing law
- b. Netting
- c. Events of default and termination
- d. The credit rating of each counterparty

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** Governing law, conditions precedent, netting, undertakings, events of default and termination are the standard heads. Credit ratings inform collateral arrangements rather than being a term of the Master Agreement itself.

</details>

### 433. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §4.5] Where a broker cannot substantiate a quotation and a difference is payable to the principal, the broker should:

- a. Close the position at the next available price and settle the difference with the principal
- b. Cancel the trade
- c. Absorb the difference without telling the principal
- d. Refer the matter to the exchange

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Close the position at the next available price and settle the difference with the principal

**Rationale:** The broker closes out at the next available price and settles the difference, which resolves the error without leaving an unhedged or disputed position open.

</details>

### 434. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §5.2] A representative recommends a product unsuitable for the client in order to meet a commission target. Which threat category does this illustrate?

- a. Self-interest
- b. Self-review
- c. Influence
- d. Competence

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Self-interest

**Rationale:** A personal interest in the commission that affects judgement is self-interest. Self-review is a lack of objectivity about one’s own work; influence is pressure from others.

</details>

### 435. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §5.4] Where the laws of two jurisdictions conflict, a representative must comply with:

- a. The stricter law, rule or regulation
- b. The law of Singapore in all cases
- c. The law of the client’s domicile
- d. The law that permits the transaction

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. The stricter law, rule or regulation

**Rationale:** The professional standard requires compliance with the stricter applicable requirement, which resolves the conflict without encouraging a search for the most permissive regime.

</details>

### 436. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §5.4] Which are exceptions to the duty of confidentiality to a client?

- a. The information concerns illegal activity by the client
- b. Disclosure is required by law
- c. The client permits disclosure
- d. A colleague on the dealing desk asks for the information

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** There are exactly three exceptions: illegal activity, legal requirement and client permission. Internal curiosity is not one of them.

</details>

### 437. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §5.4] A representative acts for a client while holding a personal position directly opposed to the client’s interest. The correct response is to:

- a. Avoid the conflict, or make full and fair disclosure before the transaction
- b. Say nothing because the client has not asked
- c. Disclose after the trade is complete
- d. Rely on the client’s general consent in the account opening form

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Avoid the conflict, or make full and fair disclosure before the transaction

**Rationale:** Conflicts must be avoided or disclosed before the transaction. Even with client consent, the representative should actively seek alternatives that avoid the conflict.

</details>

### 438. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §5.4] Which duties does a representative owe to an employer?

- a. Not to solicit the employer’s clients after resigning without permission
- b. Not to misappropriate the client list
- c. Not to engage in self-dealing
- d. To report a colleague’s market misconduct only after leaving the firm

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** Loyalty, no solicitation, no misappropriation and no self-dealing are owed to the employer. Reporting misconduct is a duty that arises immediately, and market integrity outranks employer interest.

</details>

### 439. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §5.4] A representative holds material non-public information about a security and refrains from dealing, but tells a friend who deals. The representative has:

- a. Contravened the prohibition by causing another to act on the information
- b. Acted lawfully because he did not deal himself
- c. Committed no breach because the friend decided independently
- d. Complied, provided he reports the friend afterwards

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Contravened the prohibition by causing another to act on the information

**Rationale:** The prohibition covers acting or causing others to act. Passing the information on engages the contravention as squarely as dealing on it directly.

</details>

### 440. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §5.5] Payment for order flow is prohibited because it:

- a. Creates a conflict of interest, since routing may be driven by the payment rather than by best execution
- b. Is always unprofitable for the firm
- c. Increases the client’s commission
- d. Is a form of market manipulation

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Creates a conflict of interest, since routing may be driven by the payment rather than by best execution

**Rationale:** The objection is structural: a payment linked to routing incentivises the firm to route for the fee rather than for the client’s best available terms.

</details>

### 441. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §5.5] Which statements about best execution are correct?

- a. It requires considering price, cost, speed, likelihood of execution and settlement, size and quality holistically
- b. Not executing at the best possible price is not automatically a breach
- c. Speed may be prioritised over immediate price where it is critical to the outcome
- d. Cross trades between staff personal accounts and client accounts are permitted with disclosure

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** Best execution is a process standard judged holistically. Cross trading between staff personal accounts and client accounts is prohibited outright, disclosure notwithstanding.

</details>

### 442. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §5.5] The FX Global Code is organised around:

- a. Six leading principles covering ethics, governance, information sharing, execution, risk management and compliance, and confirmation and settlement
- b. Five codes of conduct covering dealing principles, ethics, governance, back office and instruments
- c. Three principles covering price, speed and size
- d. No stated principles, only case studies

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Six leading principles covering ethics, governance, information sharing, execution, risk management and compliance, and confirmation and settlement

**Rationale:** The Code has six principles. The five-part structure describes the Blue Book, which is a common source of confusion.

</details>

### 443. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §6.2] For a client investing in equities with a long-term horizon, the analysis most suited to developing the strategy is:

- a. Fundamental analysis
- b. Technical analysis
- c. Global macro analysis
- d. Sentiment analysis

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Fundamental analysis

**Rationale:** Fundamental analysis assesses intrinsic value from profitability over time, which fits a long horizon. Technical analysis is better suited to short-term strategies.

</details>

### 444. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §6.3] Which are constraints that must be addressed when formulating a trading strategy?

- a. Time horizon of the position
- b. Liquidity needs of the client
- c. Legal restrictions on the portfolio
- d. The representative’s own commission target

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** Horizon, liquidity and legal constraints are all legitimate constraints on the strategy. The representative’s commission target is a conflict, not a client constraint.

</details>

### 445. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §6.3] Shifting portfolio weightings to take advantage of a sector expected to outperform is known as:

- a. Tactical asset allocation
- b. Strategic asset allocation
- c. Constant weighting
- d. Passive indexing

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Tactical asset allocation

**Rationale:** Tactical allocation deviates from the strategic weights to exploit a view. Strategic allocation sets the long-run policy mix.

</details>

### 446. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §6.4] Which are risk management tools available to a dealing representative?

- a. Stop-loss levels
- b. Daily loss limits
- c. Value at risk measurement
- d. Removing all limits to avoid forced exits

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** Stops, daily loss limits and VaR are the documented tools. Removing limits converts a controlled loss into an uncontrolled one.

</details>

### 447. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §6.4] A daily loss limit is reached. The representative must:

- a. Liquidate all positions or be prevented from entering new ones
- b. Continue trading to recoup the loss
- c. Increase position size to recover faster
- d. Switch to a different product

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Liquidate all positions or be prevented from entering new ones

**Rationale:** The limit exists to stop the impulse to trade out of a loss. Continuing, scaling up or switching products defeats its purpose.

</details>

### 448. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §6.6] A retail customer is deemed to have sufficient knowledge for a customer account review in respect of listed SIPs if they have transacted in listed SIPs at least:

- a. 6 times in the preceding 3 years
- b. 3 times in the preceding 5 years
- c. 10 times in the preceding 5 years
- d. Once at any time

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. 6 times in the preceding 3 years

**Rationale:** The threshold is 6 transactions in the preceding 3 years for the CAR route, and the parallel CKA route uses 6 transactions in unlisted CIS or ILPs, or in other unlisted SIPs.

</details>

### 449. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §6.6] Which may substitute for the transaction or experience route to deemed knowledge?

- a. A diploma or higher qualification in a listed discipline such as accountancy, actuarial science or finance
- b. A CFA or ACCA qualification
- c. A minimum of 3 consecutive years of relevant work experience in the past 10 years
- d. A reference letter from a former employer

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** The qualification, professional-designation and work-experience routes are all specified. A reference letter is not one of the prescribed routes.

</details>

### 450. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §6.6] The outcome of a customer account review for a listed SIP remains valid for:

- a. 3 years, extended only if the customer has transacted more than once in each 3-year period
- b. 1 year
- c. 5 years
- d. Indefinitely

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. 3 years, extended only if the customer has transacted more than once in each 3-year period

**Rationale:** The CAR is valid for 3 years, and the extension requires more than one transaction in each subsequent 3-year period. A CKA for an unlisted SIP is valid for 1 year.

</details>

### 451. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §6.6] A customer does not provide adequate information for a customer account review. The CMS licence holder must:

- a. Deem the customer not to possess knowledge or experience in derivatives
- b. Assume knowledge because the customer declined to answer
- c. Proceed if the customer is an accredited investor
- d. Obtain the information from the customer’s bank instead

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Deem the customer not to possess knowledge or experience in derivatives

**Rationale:** The default runs against the customer: inadequate information means the customer is deemed not to possess the required knowledge or experience.

</details>

### 452. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §6.7] Which statements about operational risk are correct?

- a. It arises from inadequate or failed internal processes, systems and people
- b. It is independent of financial, systematic and market-wide risk
- c. It is exemplified by the Barings collapse, where one person both dealt and settled
- d. It is another name for market risk

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** Operational risk is defined by process, system and people failure and is treated as independent of market and credit risk. Barings is the classic illustration.

</details>

### 453. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §6.7] The maximum unscheduled downtime permitted for critical IT systems of a CMS licence holder is:

- a. 4 hours within any 12-month period
- b. 4 hours per month
- c. 24 hours within any 12-month period
- d. No limit, provided MAS is notified

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. 4 hours within any 12-month period

**Rationale:** The technology risk guidance sets 4 hours of aggregate unscheduled downtime in any 12-month period, with MAS notification of a relevant incident not later than 1 hour after discovery.

</details>

### 454. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §6.7] In a margin trading account, capital risk means that the client may:

- a. Lose more than the principal invested, because the position is funded by borrowed capital
- b. Lose only the commission
- c. Never lose the principal
- d. Lose only if the broker defaults

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Lose more than the principal invested, because the position is funded by borrowed capital

**Rationale:** Leverage means losses can exceed the equity contributed. That possibility is the distinguishing feature of capital risk in a margin account.

</details>

### 455. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §6.7] Which statements about gap risk are correct?

- a. Prices move from one level to another with no trading in between
- b. A stop-loss may be executed well below the stop level because of a gap
- c. Gaps typically follow a news event
- d. A guaranteed stop removes gap risk at no cost

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** Gap risk is exactly the risk that the stop level is jumped, which is why execution can occur well beyond it. A guaranteed stop addresses this only at a cost, so the no-cost statement is false.

</details>

### 456. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §2.7] A trade is executed on a Monday with no intervening market holiday. When must the contract note be issued?

- a. By the next business day, Tuesday
- b. On the trade date
- c. Within five business days
- d. When the customer requests it

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. By the next business day, Tuesday

**Rationale:** The note is due no later than the next business day after the transaction, or the next business day after any deferred detail becomes available.

</details>

### 457. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §1.4] Which statements about the SGX-DC clearing of OTC commodity derivatives are correct?

- a. Contracts are registered through Titan OTC
- b. Clearing members post margin at rates prescribed by SGX-DC
- c. Members may set higher internal margin requirements than the minimum
- d. Variation margin is never payable

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** Variation margin is payable to reflect mark-to-market movements, so the last statement is false. Members may and should set higher internal requirements and review them continually.

</details>

### 458. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §2.3] A representative wishes to act for a second principal. This requires:

- a. MAS approval, unless the principals are related corporations
- b. No approval, provided the firms agree
- c. Written consent from the first principal only
- d. Approval from the approved exchange

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. MAS approval, unless the principals are related corporations

**Rationale:** The one-representative-one-principal rule admits an exception only with MAS approval or where the principals are related corporations.

</details>

### 459. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §2.4] Which statements about the Representative Notification Framework are correct?

- a. Notifications are lodged electronically by the principal
- b. The principal certifies that the representative is fit and proper
- c. The representative’s name appears on the Public Register of Representatives
- d. A representative’s unique number changes when they change principal

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** The unique representative number stays with the individual even when they move to a new principal, which is what makes it useful for public verification.

</details>

### 460. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §2.5] Which statement about customer confidentiality under the SFR(LCB) is correct?

- a. Information about a customer’s order may not be divulged except for effective execution, under exchange rules, or when required by MAS
- b. Confidentiality ceases when the customer relationship ends
- c. Confidentiality applies only to retail customers
- d. Confidentiality may be waived by the firm’s compliance department

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Information about a customer’s order may not be divulged except for effective execution, under exchange rules, or when required by MAS

**Rationale:** The obligation attaches to the information and endures beyond the relationship. It applies to all customers and cannot be waived by an internal approval.

</details>

### 461. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §3.4] Which are prohibited under the market conduct provisions for derivatives?

- a. Manipulating the price of a derivatives contract traded on an organised market
- b. Cornering the underlying of a derivatives contract
- c. Taking a position in the underlying for a genuine investment purpose
- d. Disseminating information about illegal transactions

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, D

**Rationale:** Manipulation, cornering and dissemination of illegal-transaction information are all prohibited. Taking a genuine position is ordinary trading.

</details>

### 462. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §3.8] A broker publishes a research note that recklessly conceals material facts in order to induce dealing. This is:

- a. Fraudulently inducing persons to deal in capital markets products
- b. Bucketing
- c. Pre-arranged trading
- d. A permitted opinion

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Fraudulently inducing persons to deal in capital markets products

**Rationale:** Reckless concealment of material facts to induce dealing falls squarely within the fraudulent-inducement provision. An honest opinion honestly held is different.

</details>

### 463. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §4.2] A CMS licence holder enters into a prescribed interest rate swap booked in Singapore. Which obligations arise?

- a. Both the reporting obligation under Part 6A and the clearing obligation under section 129C
- b. The reporting obligation only
- c. The clearing obligation only
- d. Neither, unless the counterparty is a bank

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Both the reporting obligation under Part 6A and the clearing obligation under section 129C

**Rationale:** Reporting and clearing are distinct obligations that can apply to the same contract. The counterparty’s identity does not exempt a specified person from them.

</details>

### 464. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §4.4] Which statements about risk disclosure for derivatives are correct?

- a. Form 13 must be furnished before the account is set up
- b. A client acknowledgement of the Form 13 disclosure must be obtained
- c. Established industry standards such as ISDA disclosure documents may be relied on if they meet MAS requirements
- d. Disclosure is unnecessary for an accredited investor

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** The disclosure and acknowledgement requirements apply, and industry-standard documents can satisfy them where assessed as adequate. Accredited status does not remove the requirement.

</details>

### 465. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §5.4] A representative has a personal account position in a security that a client also holds. Under the conflicts standards he must:

- a. Avoid the conflict, or make full and fair disclosure before acting, and give client orders priority
- b. Close the personal position immediately in all cases
- c. Disclose the position to the client at year end
- d. Resign from managing that client

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Avoid the conflict, or make full and fair disclosure before acting, and give client orders priority

**Rationale:** The obligation is avoidance or pre-transaction disclosure plus priority of client orders. Immediate closure and resignation are not required by the standard.

</details>

### 466. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §5.5] Which statements about payment for order flow are correct?

- a. It is prohibited for a CMS licence holder placing or executing customer orders
- b. It creates a conflict because routing may be driven by the payment rather than best execution
- c. It is permitted if disclosed to the client
- d. It is a form of commission rebate from the executing broker

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, D

**Rationale:** PFOF is prohibited outright, so disclosure does not cure it. It is a payment received for routing orders, which is precisely why the conflict arises.

</details>

### 467. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §6.6] Which statements about the approval to open a SIP trading account are correct?

- a. Approval must come from senior management not involved in that account opening
- b. The approving person must not be a connected person of the customer
- c. Approval rests on the outcome of the CAR or CKA
- d. The representative opening the account may approve it

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** The approval must be independent of the account-opening process and of the customer, and must rest on the assessment outcome. The representative cannot approve their own account opening.

</details>

### 468. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §6.6] A customer is assessed as not possessing knowledge of unlisted SIPs but insists on proceeding. The firm may transact on the customer’s behalf only if:

- a. The firm is also an exempt financial adviser and provides advice in accordance with FAA-N16
- b. The customer signs a waiver
- c. The customer is an accredited investor
- d. The amount is below S$50,000

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. The firm is also an exempt financial adviser and provides advice in accordance with FAA-N16

**Rationale:** For an unlisted SIP the firm must be an exempt financial adviser to proceed, and must then advise to the FAA-N16 standard. A waiver, accredited status or a size threshold does not substitute.

</details>

### 469. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §6.7] Which statements about CRAFT and MAS supervision are correct?

- a. CRAFT is a risk-based supervisory framework using an impact and risk model
- b. MAS prioritises supervisory resources toward firms posing the greatest potential impact
- c. Diagnostic tools identify problems at an early stage
- d. Remedial and diagnostic tools operate independently and never need to complement each other

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** Diagnostic and remedial work must complement each other: diagnosis establishes whether a risk is material, and remedial action sets a time-bound plan to mitigate it.

</details>

### 470. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §6.7] A firm’s systems fail and it cannot confirm trades for several hours. Which risk category does this primarily illustrate?

- a. Operational risk
- b. Market risk
- c. Credit risk
- d. Regulatory risk

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Operational risk

**Rationale:** A failure of systems and processes is operational risk by definition. It may give rise to other risks, but the originating category is operational.

</details>

### 471. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §6.7] Which statements about liquidity risk for a dealing representative are correct?

- a. It can arise when closing out a position in a thinly traded product
- b. Bid-ask spreads typically widen when trading volume is low
- c. It is eliminated by holding the position to expiry
- d. Trading more liquid, less volatile derivatives can reduce it

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, D

**Rationale:** Liquidity risk crystallises on exit, and thinner markets widen spreads. Holding to expiry does not remove the risk; it defers the exit.

</details>

### 472. **[Scenario]** [RES 2B §3.16] A trader executes as many trades as possible in a small client account with no sound market rationale, generating substantial commission. Which offence best describes this?

- a. Churning
- b. Bucketing
- c. Pre-arranged trading
- d. Wash trading

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Churning

**Rationale:** Churning is excessive trading for the purpose of generating commission rather than serving the client’s interest.

</details>

### 473. **[Scenario]** [RES 2B §2.8] A firm deposits retail customer money for OTC derivatives into its own operating account overnight, then transfers it to a trust account the next morning. Is this acceptable?

- a. No; retail customer money for OTC derivatives must be deposited into a trust account
- b. Yes; next-business-day deposit meets the requirement
- c. Yes, if the money is restored by the same time next day
- d. No, unless the customer consents to the operating account

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. No; retail customer money for OTC derivatives must be deposited into a trust account

**Rationale:** The next-business-day rule governs the timing of deposit, not the destination. Money for retail OTC derivatives must go into a trust account, so an operating account is never a permitted destination.

</details>

### 474. **[Scenario]** [RES 2B §6.6] A customer completed a CAR for listed SIPs two years ago and has not traded since. May the firm let him trade a listed SIP today?

- a. Yes, because the CAR remains valid for three years from the date it was conducted
- b. No, because a CAR lapses after one year
- c. No, because he has not traded in the last year
- d. Only if he signs a fresh risk disclosure

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Yes, because the CAR remains valid for three years from the date it was conducted

**Rationale:** The CAR is valid for three years. The extension beyond three years requires more than one transaction in each three-year period, but that condition only bites at the three-year mark.

</details>

### 475. **[Multiple response]** [RES 12B Final revision checklist] Which distinctions must be clear before sitting the paper?

- a. A customer account review from a customer knowledge assessment
- b. An excluded investment product from a specified investment product
- c. Customer money from customer assets
- d. A trading member from a clearing member

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C, D

**Rationale:** All four pairs are fundamental: CAR and CKA address listed and unlisted products respectively; EIP and SIP determine whether the assessment applies at all; money and assets are held under different rules; and membership of an exchange is not membership of a clearing house.

</details>

### 476. **[Single choice]** [RES 12B Final revision checklist] Which pair is most often confused because both concern product complexity?

- a. An excluded investment product and a specified investment product
- b. A trading member and a clearing member
- c. Placement and layering
- d. A trust account and a custody account

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. An excluded investment product and a specified investment product

**Rationale:** The EIP and SIP boundary decides whether a CAR or CKA is needed at all, so misclassifying a product removes the whole assessment. The other pairs are distinct in a way that is usually self-evident from context.

</details>

### 477. **[Multiple response]** [RES 12B Final revision checklist] Which conduct offences turn on the absence of a genuine commercial purpose rather than on the price achieved?

- a. False trading
- b. Wash trading
- c. Bucketing
- d. Pre-arranged trading

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C, D

**Rationale:** Each is defined by purpose or by the absence of a bona fide market transaction: false trading by the intention to mislead, wash trading by the absence of economic effect, bucketing by the absence of any market execution, and pre-arranged trading by the predetermined price.

</details>

### 478. **[Single choice]** [RES 12B Final revision checklist] Which risk category is defined by failed internal processes, systems and people rather than by market or credit events?

- a. Operational risk
- b. Market risk
- c. Liquity risk
- d. Regulatory risk

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Operational risk

**Rationale:** Operational risk is defined by process, system and people failure and is independent of market and credit risk. The Barings collapse is the standard illustration.

</details>

### 479. **[Multiple response]** [RES 12B Final revision checklist] Which are the stages of money laundering?

- a. Placement
- b. Layering
- c. Integration
- d. Confiscation

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** Placement, layering and integration are the three stages. Confiscation is a legal consequence of detection, not a stage of the process.

</details>

### 480. **[Single choice]** [RES 12B Final revision checklist] Which best distinguishes a customer account review from a customer knowledge assessment?

- a. The CAR applies to listed SIPs and the CKA to unlisted SIPs
- b. The CAR applies to retail clients and the CKA to accredited clients
- c. The CAR is optional and the CKA is mandatory
- d. The CAR is conducted by MAS and the CKA by the firm

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. The CAR applies to listed SIPs and the CKA to unlisted SIPs

**Rationale:** The dividing line is the product, not the client: listed SIPs require a CAR and unlisted SIPs require a CKA. Both are conducted by the firm.

</details>

### 481. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §3.5] Which element distinguishes market manipulation under section 198 from ordinary trading that happens to move a price?

- a. Intentional interference with the free forces of supply and demand to deceive or defraud
- b. The size of the position taken relative to the market
- c. Execution on an organised market rather than over the counter
- d. The profit actually realised by the trader

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Intentional interference with the free forces of supply and demand to deceive or defraud

**Rationale:** Market manipulation is defined by intentional interference with the free forces of supply and demand to deceive or defraud investors, or for other ulterior purposes. Size and realised profit are not the test.

</details>

### 482. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §3.6] A representative circulates an unverified rumour about a counterparty’s funding position. Which mental element must accompany a false or misleading statement for the prohibition to be engaged?

- a. Not caring whether it is true or false, or knowing or having reason to know it is false or misleading
- b. Intending to profit from the statement
- c. Communicating it to more than one person
- d. Repeating it after it has been denied

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Not caring whether it is true or false, or knowing or having reason to know it is false or misleading

**Rationale:** The prohibition applies where the person does not care whether the information is true or false, or knows or should have known that it is false or misleading in a material particular. Dissemination can be by newsletter, research paper, electronic means, media or word of mouth.

</details>

### 483. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §3.9] Why does the SFA contain a general provision on employing manipulative and deceptive devices when specific offences such as false trading already exist?

- a. Because the devices perpetrators may use cannot all be defined in advance, so a catch-all is needed
- b. Because the specific offences apply only to securities and not to derivatives
- c. Because the specific offences require proof of profit
- d. Because the catch-all carries a lower penalty and is easier to prove

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Because the devices perpetrators may use cannot all be defined in advance, so a catch-all is needed

**Rationale:** The SFA provides a general catch-all precisely because it is not possible to define every device perpetrators may employ. The Madoff Ponzi scheme is the guide’s illustration.

</details>

### 484. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §3.9] Which acts fall within the prohibition on employing manipulative and deceptive devices in connection with a transaction in a capital markets product?

- a. Employing any device, scheme or artifice to defraud
- b. Engaging in an act or course of business that operates or is likely to operate as a fraud or deception
- c. Making a statement the person knows to be false in a material particular
- d. Omitting a material fact necessary to make the statements made not misleading

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C, D

**Rationale:** All four limbs are stated in the provision. The omission limb matters because a statement can be misleading by what it leaves out rather than by what it says.

</details>

### 485. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §3.11] Person Z arranges for another to influence a share price by deceptive methods, then tells third parties so they can trade on the expected move, expecting a share of their profits. Which offence does this describe?

- a. Dissemination of information about illegal transactions
- b. Bucketing
- c. Front running
- d. Pre-arranged trading

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Dissemination of information about illegal transactions

**Rationale:** Circulating information about illegal transactions is an offence in its own right, because trading on the expected move can create or exacerbate the artificial conditions without the trader committing the underlying manipulation.

</details>

### 486. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §3.13] A representative holds back part of a customer’s order so that a favoured client can trade ahead of it. Which prohibition is engaged?

- a. Withholding or withdrawing a customer’s order for the benefit of the holder or another person
- b. Churning
- c. Cornering
- d. Cross trading

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Withholding or withdrawing a customer’s order for the benefit of the holder or another person

**Rationale:** Regulation 47(1) prohibits withholding or withdrawing from an organised market any order, or part of an order, for the holder’s own benefit or to benefit any other person.

</details>

### 487. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §3.13] Which statements about a customer’s order information are correct under the SFR(LCB)?

- a. It must be kept confidential
- b. It may be disclosed where necessary for the effective execution of the order
- c. It may be disclosed where permitted under the rules of the relevant exchange or clearing house
- d. It may be disclosed where MAS requires it under the SFA or SFR(LCB)

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C, D

**Rationale:** Regulation 47(2) permits disclosure only on those three grounds, alongside the general confidentiality obligation. There is no ground of commercial convenience or customer relationship value.

</details>

### 488. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §3.14] A CMS licence holder wants to take the opposite side of a retail customer’s futures order for its own account. What is required?

- a. The customer’s prior consent, and compliance with the exchange’s business rules and practices
- b. Nothing, because a principal may always trade against its own customer
- c. A written risk disclosure signed by the customer after the trade
- d. Approval from MAS before the transaction

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. The customer’s prior consent, and compliance with the exchange’s business rules and practices

**Rationale:** Regulation 47C(1) permits the firm to buy from or sell to its customer only with the customer’s prior consent and in accordance with the business rules and practices of an approved exchange or recognised market operator.

</details>

### 489. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B §3.18] A representative maintains client positions that exceed the limits imposed by the sponsoring member or the exchange. What does the guide identify this as?

- a. Overtrading, for which the holder is responsible even as to customers’ positions
- b. Churning, because it generates commission
- c. Bucketing, because the orders were not exposed
- d. Pre-arranged trading, because the limits were agreed in advance

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Overtrading, for which the holder is responsible even as to customers’ positions

**Rationale:** Candidates are prohibited from executing trades or maintaining positions that breach limits, and are responsible for keeping customers’ positions within the prescribed limits. A breach may be notified to members, and steps must be taken to reduce the excess.

</details>

### 490. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §3.18] Which statements about position limits are correct?

- a. Limits may be imposed by sponsoring members, approved exchanges, clearing houses or regulatory authorities
- b. The firm is responsible for keeping customers’ positions within the prescribed limits
- c. A limit breach may be notified by the exchange or clearing house to its members
- d. A limit breach is cured by later reducing the position, so no notification is required

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** All three of the first statements are correct. Reducing an excess position is required, but it does not remove the breach or the notification obligation.

</details>

### 491. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B §3.20] Beyond criminal and civil penalties, which consequences does the guide identify for market misconduct?

- a. Civil liability to compensate persons who traded similarly at the time and suffered losses
- b. Reputational loss, including loss of customer confidence
- c. Suspension or revocation of the firm’s licence or the representative’s registration
- d. Automatic transfer of the representative’s client accounts to another firm

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** The four consequence categories are criminal penalties, civil penalties, civil liability with financial and reputational loss, and suspension or loss of licence or registration. Client transfers are not among them.

</details>

## RES 2B — Appendices

### 492. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B Appendix A] In the criteria for assessment of a customer account review, which of the following is relevant to the assessment?

- a. The customer’s educational qualifications, investment experience and work experience
- b. The customer’s marital status
- c. The customer’s residential address
- d. The customer’s preferred payment method

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. The customer’s educational qualifications, investment experience and work experience

**Rationale:** The CAR takes account of education, investment experience and work experience. The other items have no bearing on knowledge or experience.

</details>

### 493. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B Appendix B] For a customer to satisfy the customer knowledge assessment in respect of unlisted specified investment products, the qualifying transaction count refers to:

- a. Unlisted CIS or ILPs, or other unlisted SIPs, at least 6 times in the preceding 3 years
- b. Listed SIPs at least 6 times in the preceding 3 years
- c. Any investment product at least 10 times in the preceding 5 years
- d. A single transaction of any size

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Unlisted CIS or ILPs, or other unlisted SIPs, at least 6 times in the preceding 3 years

**Rationale:** The CKA route counts unlisted CIS or ILP transactions, or other unlisted SIP transactions, at least 6 times in the preceding 3 years. Counting listed SIPs describes the CAR route.

</details>

### 494. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B Appendix C] Which risks must the overseas-listed investment product risk warning statement draw to the customer’s attention?

- a. Differences in the level of investor protection in the foreign jurisdiction
- b. Differences between the foreign legal system and Singapore’s, including the difficulty of predicting outcomes
- c. The possibility that exchange controls restrict repatriation of funds
- d. A guarantee that the foreign exchange will compensate for any loss

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** The statement warns about regulatory, legal and repatriation differences and counterparty and correspondent broker risk. No compensation guarantee exists.

</details>

### 495. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B Appendix C] The overseas-listed product risk warning statement requirement is found in:

- a. Paragraph 29D of the MAS Notice on Sale of Investment Products (SFA 04-N12)
- b. Section 129C of the SFA
- c. Part 6A of the SFA
- d. The CDP Clearing Rules

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. Paragraph 29D of the MAS Notice on Sale of Investment Products (SFA 04-N12)

**Rationale:** Paragraph 29D of SFA 04-N12 imposes the warning and acknowledgement requirement. Section 129C concerns clearing and Part 6A concerns derivatives reporting.

</details>

### 496. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B Appendix D] Which of the following is an excluded investment product?

- a. An ordinary share listed on an exchange
- b. A structured note
- c. An asset-backed security
- d. A collective investment scheme with a broad mandate

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. An ordinary share listed on an exchange

**Rationale:** Ordinary shares, share units and business trust units are EIPs. Structured notes and asset-backed securities are expressly carved out of the debenture limb, so both are SIPs.

</details>

### 497. **[Multiple response]** [RES 2B Appendix D] Which statements about excluded investment products are correct?

- a. Dealing in an EIP does not require a customer account review
- b. Debentures are EIPs except asset-backed securities and structured notes
- c. Only an immediate currency exchange is an EIP; a forward or swap is not
- d. Stapling an EIP to a SIP produces an EIP

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A, B, C

**Rationale:** A staple qualifies only where the stapled products are themselves EIPs, so stapling to a SIP does not confer EIP status.

</details>

### 498. **[Single choice]** [RES 2B Appendix E] The list of suspicious transaction examples is described in the guidance as:

- a. An aid to judgement that must not be applied as a routine instrument in place of common sense
- b. An exhaustive and definitive list of offences
- c. A substitute for customer due diligence
- d. A list that applies only to banks

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. An aid to judgement that must not be applied as a routine instrument in place of common sense

**Rationale:** The list is expressly an aid, non-exhaustive, and not a substitute for judgement. A combination of indicators is what makes a transaction genuinely suspicious.

</details>

## RES 1B — Appendices

### 499. **[Single choice]** [RES 1B Appendix A] Which document is the source of the fit and proper criteria for representatives?

- a. The MAS Guidelines on Fit and Proper Criteria (FSG-01)
- b. The MAS Guidelines on Criteria for the Grant of a CMS Licence (SFA 04-G01)
- c. The MAS Notice on Reporting of Misconduct of Representatives (04-N11)
- d. The SFA itself, without reference to any guideline

<details>
<summary>Show answer</summary>

**Answer:** A. The MAS Guidelines on Fit and Proper Criteria (FSG-01)

**Rationale:** FSG-01 sets the fit and proper criteria. SFA 04-G01 concerns licensing criteria for the firm, and 04-N11 concerns misconduct reporting.

</details>
